# DAXDA.IA — Nicole Protocol
## Full 886-Ops Architectural Structure

**Generated:** 2026-05-17 08:50:25

> This Markdown file defines the full DAXDA.IA / Nicole Protocol architecture as an 886-operation governance system.
> The structure is intentionally operational: each tile has a layer, function, purpose, input, output, and gate behavior.

---

## 0. Correction and Operating Definition

The requested count is **886 operations**. Since 886 does not divide evenly across 16 layers, this architecture uses:

- **16 governed layers × 55 operations per layer = 880 layer operations**
- **6 post-layer system authority operations = 886 total operations**

This preserves the existing 16-layer DAXDA.IA live-pipeline structure while making the operation budget explicit and auditable.

---

## 1. System Identity

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| System | DAXDA.IA |
| Protocol | Nicole Protocol |
| Architecture Type | Recursive governed reasoning pipeline |
| Layer Count | 16 |
| Layer Operations | 880 |
| Post-Layer System Operations | 6 |
| Total Operations | 886 |
| Primary Gate States | PASS, WARN, CAUTION, RECURSE, BLOCK, RELEASE |
| Primary Output Authority | Advisory unless promoted by evidence and governance gates |

---

## 2. Top-Level Execution Flow

```text
USER INPUT
  ↓
FDL_1 — Frame Detection Layer
  ↓
AML_1 — Assumption Map Layer
  ↓
AWP_1 — Claim Weighting Layer
  ↓
BST_1 — Breaker Stress Test Layer
  ↓
CRL_1 — Claim Recovery Layer
  ↓
MCS_1 — Monte Carlo Simulator Layer
  ↓
DSV_1 — Decision Valve Layer
  ↓
TRC_1 — Trace Consistency Layer
  ↓
CON_1 — Contradiction Scan Layer
  ↓
EVD_1 — Evidence Demand Layer
  ↓
REC_1 — Recursion Plan Layer
  ↓
GOV_1 — Governance Gate Layer
  ↓
OUT_1 — Output Record Layer
  ↓
RIL_1 — Recursion Integrity Layer
  ↓
IAL_1 — Incentive Alignment Layer
  ↓
AOG_1 — Authority Output Gate Layer
  ↓
SYS_881 → SYS_886 — Final scoring, authority, manifest, release lock, re-entry, memory boundary
  ↓
GOVERNED OUTPUT
```

---

## 3. Layer Registry

| # | Layer ID | Layer Name | Core Function |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | `FDL_1` | Frame Detection Layer | Detects the actual frame: claims, questions, constraints, intent, domain, and success boundaries. |
| 2 | `AML_1` | Assumption Map Layer | Exposes explicit, implicit, hidden, fragile, and load-bearing assumptions. |
| 3 | `AWP_1` | Claim Weighting Layer | Weights claims by confidence, risk, dependency, priority, evidence demand, and action relevance. |
| 4 | `BST_1` | Breaker Stress Test Layer | Attacks claims with counterexamples, adversarial pressure, edge cases, and failure-mode discovery. |
| 5 | `CRL_1` | Claim Recovery Layer | Repairs surviving claims without hiding failure, inflating confidence, or collapsing ambiguity. |
| 6 | `MCS_1` | Monte Carlo Simulator Layer | Simulates likely, edge, hostile, low-data, and failure scenarios to estimate survivability. |
| 7 | `DSV_1` | Decision Valve Layer | Converts survivability and risk state into proceed, refine, recurse, defer, caution, or block. |
| 8 | `TRC_1` | Trace Consistency Layer | Checks that the reasoning trail remains connected across input, claims, evidence, assumptions, and output. |
| 9 | `CON_1` | Contradiction Scan Layer | Detects direct contradictions, soft conflicts, assumption collisions, and severity. |
| 10 | `EVD_1` | Evidence Demand Layer | Lists what evidence is required before authority, confidence, or action permission may rise. |
| 11 | `REC_1` | Recursion Plan Layer | Defines targeted re-entry, refinement payloads, split paths, and next-pass requirements. |
| 12 | `GOV_1` | Governance Gate Layer | Applies safety, legitimacy, privacy, legality, authority, and operational-risk controls. |
| 13 | `OUT_1` | Output Record Layer | Builds the governed answer, summary, recommendations, uncertainty disclosure, and output metadata. |
| 14 | `RIL_1` | Recursion Integrity Layer | Detects fake recursion, loop collapse, shallow repetition, low information gain, and rubric theater. |
| 15 | `IAL_1` | Incentive Alignment Layer | Penalizes reward gaming, unsupported confidence, hidden optimization, and performative scoring. |
| 16 | `AOG_1` | Authority Output Gate Layer | Assigns final authority level, permission state, audit readiness, release state, and re-entry hooks. |

---

## 4. Universal Tile Schema

Each operation tile follows this schema:

```yaml
tile_id: DAX_<layer_number>_<operation_number>
layer_id: <layer code>
layer_name: <layer name>
function_name: <atomic function>
purpose: <why this tile exists>
input_contract:
  - active_user_input
  - upstream_layer_state
  - evidence_state
  - constraint_state
output_contract:
  - tile_result
  - confidence_delta
  - warning_delta
  - trace_entry
gate_behavior:
  pass: result is coherent, supported, and traceable
  warn: result is usable but weakened
  caution: result requires caveat or refinement
  recurse: result must be re-entered into prior layer
  block: result cannot be safely or honestly released
```

---

## 5. Global Scoring Model

```text
TOTAL_STABILITY =
  (wL × Logic)
+ (wA × Assumption Integrity)
+ (wP × Practicality)
+ (wF × Falsifiability)
+ (wT × Traceability)
- (wX × Black Box Penalty)
- (wR × Risk Penalty)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| L | Logical coherence |
| A | Assumption integrity |
| P | Practical viability |
| F | Falsifiability / testability |
| T | Traceability / auditability |
| X | Black-box opacity penalty |
| R | Residual risk penalty |

---

## 6. Gate State Definitions

| Gate | Meaning | Required Behavior |
|---|---|---|
| PASS | Strong enough to proceed | Release layer output with trace |
| WARN | Usable but weakened | Release with warning and uncertainty note |
| CAUTION | Material ambiguity or volatility | Release only with caveats or refined framing |
| RECURSE | Current state is insufficient | Build recursion payload and re-enter targeted layer |
| BLOCK | Unsafe, unsupported, or invalid | Do not release final claim/action |
| RELEASE | Final authority gate cleared | Emit governed output and audit manifest |

---

## 9. Function Registry

These 55 atomic functions are reused in every governed layer, producing 880 layer operations.

|---:|---|---|
| 1 | `intake_snapshot` | Capture the active input, task state, prior context, source context, and timestamp. |
| 2 | `normalize_input` | Normalize wording, structure, references, symbols, and format without changing meaning. |
| 3 | `domain_classify` | Classify the domain, stakes, volatility, and likely need for external grounding. |
| 4 | `entity_extract` | Identify people, systems, files, concepts, tools, dates, locations, and named objects. |
| 5 | `claim_extract` | Extract factual claims, implied claims, assertions, hypotheses, and positions. |

| 7 | `goal_extract` | Extract practical goal, strategic goal, emotional goal, success criteria, and failure state. |

## 10. Runtime Modes

| Mode | Operation Budget | Use Case |
|---|---:|---|
| Quick Diagnostic | 128 | Fast UI pass, low-stakes inspection |
| Standard Governance | 256–512 | Serious analysis with warnings and trace |
| DAXDA 886 | 886 | Full 16-layer atomic governance pass |
| DAXDA 896 Compatibility | 896 | 886 core + 10 integration/transport ops |
| DA-13² Deep Recursion | 7,280+ | Research-grade recursive stress testing |

---

## 11. Recommended Implementation Files

```text
daxda/
  core/
    engine.py
    layers.py
    tiles.py
    scoring.py
    gates.py
    recursion.py
    audit.py
  schemas/
    daxda_886.schema.json
    layer_output.schema.json
    tile_output.schema.json
  api/
    main.py
    routes.py
    models.py
  ui/
    LayerCard.tsx
    OpTileGrid.tsx
    GovernanceGate.tsx
    AuditTrace.tsx
    StabilityPanel.tsx
```

---

## 12. Practical Build Notes

- Treat each tile as a callable function with a standardized input/output contract.
- Never let a layer silently mutate the state. Every mutation must emit a trace entry.
- Warnings must propagate forward. A warning cannot disappear unless a later layer explicitly resolves it.
- A CAUTION gate with zero warnings is semantically inconsistent unless the caution is produced only by threshold math.
- Black-box penalty should increase whenever a result lacks trace, evidence, falsification criteria, or dependency binding.
- The 886 build should be described as **full atomic governance mode**, not a casual chat mode.

---

## 13. Minimal Runtime Pseudocode

```python
def run_daxda_886(input_payload):
    state = initialize_state(input_payload)
    for layer in LAYERS_16:
        for tile in layer.tiles_55:
            result = tile.execute(state)
            state = apply_tile_result(state, result)
            state.audit.append(result.trace_entry)
            if result.gate == 'BLOCK':
                return blocked_output(state)
            if result.gate == 'RECURSE':
                state = run_targeted_recursion(state, result.recursion_payload)
    state = run_post_layer_system_ops(state)
    return governed_output(state)
```

---

## 14. Final Definition

**DAXDA.IA 886-Ops Mode** is a 16-layer recursive governance runtime in which each layer executes 55 atomic audit tiles, followed by 6 final system authority operations. Its purpose is not merely to produce an answer, but to produce a traceable, stress-tested, evidence-aware, contradiction-scanned, recursively governable answer with explicit authority boundaries.
